### mailslurp-client

Official MailSlurp Python SDK Email API

`pip install mailslurp-client` · installs (wheel) · licence: not stated · rank 9080 · last release 2026-10-26 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/mailslurp-client/)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

> Create real email addresses on demand. Send and receive emails and attachments from code and tests using Python.

MailSlurp is an email API service that lets you create real email addresses in code. You can then send and receive emails and attachments in Python applications and tests.

Example:
```python
import mailslurp_client

configuration = mailslurp_client.Configuration()
configuration.api_key["x-api-key"] = YOUR_API_KEY
```

Sections: MailSlurp Python Client, Video tutorial, Quick links, Get started, Create API Key, Configure, Email usage examples, Create an email address, Access inbox using SMTP, Send with SMTP client, List inboxes, Get an inbox, Delete an inbox, Upload attachments

</details>

### model-index

Create a source of truth for ML model results and browse it on Papers with Code

`pip install model-index` · installs (wheel) · licence: MIT · rank 9179 · last release 2021-03-22 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/model-index/)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

`model-index` has two goals:
- Make it easy to maintain a source-of-truth index of Machine Learning model metadata
- Enable the community browse this model metadata on Papers with Code

The main design principle of `model-index` is flexibility. ...

Sections: model-index: maintain a source of truth for ML models, How it works, Storing metadata in markdown files, Get started, Uploading to Papers with Code

### mineru-vl-utils

Utilities for MinerU Vision-Language models

`pip install mineru-vl-utils` · installs (wheel) · licence: Apache-2.0 · rank 9400 · last release 2026-10-29 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/mineru-vl-utils/)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

A Python package for interacting with the MinerU Vision-Language Model.

It's a lightweight wrapper that simplifies the process of sending requests
and handling responses from the MinerU Vision-Language Model.

We provides 7 different backends(deployment modes):

1. ...

from mineru_vl_utils import MinerUClient

client = MinerUClient(
    backend="http-client",
    server_url="
)

extracted_blocks = client.two_step_extract(image)
print(extracted_blocks)

> [!IMPORTANT]

from markitdown import MarkItDown

`pip install marshmallow-fastoneofschema` · installs (wheel) · licence: MIT License · rank 9916 · last release 2025-09-02 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/marshmallow-fastoneofschema/) · [source](https://github.com/Kalepa/marshmallow-fastoneofschema)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

An extension to marshmallow to support fast schema (de)multiplexing.

marshmallow is a fantastic library for serialization and deserialization of data.
For more on that project see its GitHub
page or its Documentation.

This library adds a special kind of schema that actually multiplexes other schemas
based on object type. ...

Example:
```python
import marshmallow
import marshmallow.fields
from marshmallow_fastoneofschema import OneOfSchema

class Foo:
    def init(self, foo):
        self.foo = foo

class Bar:
    def init(self, bar):
        self.bar = bar

class FooSchema(marshmallow.Schema):
    foo = marshmallow.fields.String(required=True)

    @marshmallow.post_load
...
```

...

</details>

### macaddress

Like ``ipaddress``, but for hardware identifiers such as MAC addresses.

`pip install macaddress` · installs (wheel) · licence: 0BSD · rank 10034 · last release 2022-11-04 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/macaddress/)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

A module for handling hardware identifiers like MAC addresses.

This module makes it easy to:

1. check if a string represents a valid MAC address, or a similar
 hardware identifier like an EUI-64, OUI, etc,

2. convert between string and binary forms of MAC addresses and
 other hardware identifiers,

and so on. ...

```python
>>> import macaddress
```

### mypy-strict-kwargs

Enforce using keyword arguments where possible.

`pip install mypy-strict-kwargs` · installs (wheel) · licence: MIT License · rank 10307 · last release 2026-10-19 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/mypy-strict-kwargs/) · [docs](https://mypy-strict-kwargs.readthedocs.io/en/latest/) · [source](https://github.com/adamtheturtle/mypy-strict-kwargs)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

Enforce using keyword arguments where possible.

For example, if we have a function which takes two regular argument, there are three ways to call it.
With this plugin, `mypy` will only accept the form where keyword arguments are used.

Features:
- In the same spirit as a formatter - think `black` or `ruff format` - this lets you stop spending time discussing whether a function call should use keyword arguments. ...

Example:
```python
"""Showcase errors when calling a function without naming the arguments."""

def add(a: int, b: int) -> int:
    """Add two numbers."""
    return a + b

_ = add(a=1, b=2)  # With this plugin, `mypy` will only accept this form
_ = add(1, 2)  # type: ignore[call-arg]
_ = add(1, b=2)  # type: ignore[call-arg]
```

...

</details>

### mypy-pytest-parametrize

Check pytest and Karva parametrized values against test annotations.

`pip install mypy-pytest-parametrize` · installs (wheel) · licence: MIT License · rank 10622 · last release 2026-10-05 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/mypy-pytest-parametrize/) · [docs](https://github.com/adamtheturtle/mypy-pytest-parametrize#readme) · [source](https://github.com/adamtheturtle/mypy-pytest-parametrize)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

Check `pytest.mark.parametrize` and `karva.tags.parametrize` values against test annotations.

For example, this plugin reports the `float` passed to a parameter annotated as `int`:

Features:
`pytest` accepts parametrized values without checking them against the test's annotations.
This plugin lets `mypy` find incompatible values before the tests run.
It supports multiple parameters, stacked decorators, and inline `pytest.param` calls.

Example:
```python
"""Showcase errors in pytest parametrized values."""

import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    argnames="arg",
    argvalues=[
        123,
        456.789,  # type: ignore[pytest-parametrize]
    ],
)
def test_foo(arg: int) -> None:
    """Check a parametrized integer."""
    assert arg > 0
```

...

</details>

### markitdown-ocr

OCR plugin for MarkItDown - Extracts text from images in PDF, DOCX, PPTX, and XLSX via LLM Vision

`pip install markitdown-ocr` · installs (wheel) · licence: MIT · rank 10772 · last release 2026-10-21 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/markitdown-ocr/) · [docs](https://github.com/microsoft/markitdown#readme) · [source](https://github.com/microsoft/markitdown)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

LLM Vision plugin for MarkItDown that extracts text from images embedded in PDF, DOCX, PPTX, and XLSX files.

Uses the same `llm_client` / `llm_model` pattern that MarkItDown already supports for image descriptions — no new ML libraries or binary dependencies required.

Features:
- Enhanced PDF Converter: Extracts text from images within PDFs, with full-page OCR fallback for scanned documents
- Enhanced DOCX Converter: OCR for images in Word documents
- Enhanced PPTX Converter: OCR for images in PowerPoint presentations ...

Example:
```python
from markitdown import MarkItDown
from openai import OpenAI

md = MarkItDown(
    enable_plugins=True,
    llm_client=OpenAI(),
    llm_model="gpt-4o",
)

result = md.convert("document_with_images.pdf")
print(result.text_content)
```

...

### mineru

A practical document parsing tool for converting PDF, OFD, EPUB, HTML, images, CSV, RTF, OOXML, and OpenDocument files into Markdown and JSON

`pip install mineru` · installs (wheel) · licence: LicenseRef-MinerU-Open-Source-License · rank 11046 · last release 2026-11-04 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/mineru/) · [docs](https://opendatalab.github.io/MinerU/) · [source](https://github.com/opendatalab/MinerU)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

name: mineru
description: Use MinerU as the preferred tool for reading, parsing, OCR, searching, inspecting, and citing documents. Support parsing PDF, scanned/captured document images, .doc/.docx, .ppt/.pptx, .xls/.xlsx, .rtf, .odt/.ods/.odp, .epub, .ofd, .html/.htm, .mhtml/.mht, and .csv files. ...

Example:
```python
import time

from mineru import DoclibClient
from mineru.doclib import ParseRequest

client = DoclibClient()
submit = client.ensure_parse(ParseRequest(path="paper.pdf", tier="standard"))

# ensure_parse returns immediately; poll the parse tasks it created.
for parse_id in submit.wait_parse_ids:
    parse = client.get_parse(parse_id)
...
```

...

</details>

### mulpyplexer

A module that multiplexes interactions with lists of python objects.

`pip install mulpyplexer` · installs from source (sdist only) · licence: BSD · rank 11516 · last release 2021-01-11 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/mulpyplexer/)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

Mulpyplexer is a piece of code that can multiplex interactions with lists of python objects.
It's easier to show you:

 import mulpyplexer

 class A:
 def init(self, i):
 self.i = i

 def add(self, j):
 return A(self.i + (j.i if isinstance(j, A) else j))

 def sub(self, j):
 return A(self.i - (j.i if isinstance(j, A) else j)) ...

</details>

### mercapi

Python API for querying and browsing mercari.jp

`pip install mercapi` · installs (wheel) · licence: MIT License · rank 11523 · last release 2026-09-12 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/mercapi/) · [source](https://github.com/take-kun/mercapi/)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

API Documentation

Mercapi is a Python wrapper for *mercari.jp* API.
It's capable of producing HTTP requests implementing security mechanisms employed in native *mercari.jp* web app.
Requests and responses are mapped to custom classes with type-hinting and documentation.

Example:
```python
from mercapi import Mercapi

m = Mercapi()
results = await m.search('sharpnel')

print(f'Found {results.meta.num_found} results')
for item in results.items:
    print(f'Name: {item.name}\\nPrice: {item.price}\\n')
```

Sections: mercapi, What is Mercapi?, Quickstart

</details>

### mhfp

Molecular MHFP fingerprints for cheminformatics applications

`pip install mhfp` · installs (wheel) · licence: not stated · rank 11746 · last release 2023-02-16 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/mhfp/)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

MHFP6 (MinHash fingerprint, up to six bonds) is a molecular fingerprint which encodes detailed substructures using the extended connectivity principle of ECFP in a fundamentally different manner, increasing the performance of exact nearest neighbor searches in benchmarking studies and enabling the application of locality sensitive hashing (LSH) ...

Example:
```python
from mhfp.encoder import MHFPEncoder

mhfp_encoder = MHFPEncoder()

fp_a = mhfp_encoder.encode('CCOC1=C(C=C(C=C1)S(=O)(=O)N(C)C)C2=NC(=O)C3=C(N2)C(=NN3C)C(C)(C)C')
fp_b = mhfp_encoder.encode('CCCC1=NN(C2=C1NC(=NC2=O)C3=C(C=CC(=C3)S(=O)(=O)N4CCN(CC4)C)OCC)C')
fp_c = mhfp_encoder.encode('O=C(OC)C(C1CCCCN1)C2=CC=CC=C2')

...
```

### meilisearch-python-sdk

A Python client providing both async and sync support for the Meilisearch API

`pip install meilisearch-python-sdk` · installs (wheel) · licence: MIT License · rank 11794 · last release 2026-11-04 · [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/meilisearch-python-sdk/) · [docs](https://meilisearch-python-sdk.paulsanders.dev) · [source](https://github.com/sanders41/meilisearch-python-sdk)

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

Meilisearch Python SDK provides both an async and sync client for the
Meilisearch API.

Which client to use depends on your use case. If the code base you are working with uses asyncio,
for example if you are using FastAPI, choose the `AsyncClient`,
otherwise choose the sync `Client`. ...

Example:
```python
from meilisearch_python_sdk import AsyncClient

async with AsyncClient(' 'masterKey') as client:
    index = client.index("books")

    documents = [
        {"id": 1, "title": "Ready Player One"},
        {"id": 42, "title": "The Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy"},
    ]

    await index.add_documents(documents)
```

...

</details>

from materialyoucolor.scheme import Scheme
from materialyoucolor.scheme.scheme_android import SchemeAndroid

# Color is a an int, which is made as:
# 0xff + hex_code (without #)
# Eg: 0xff + #4181EE = 0xff4181EE
# To convert hex to this form, do `int("0xff" + "", 16)`
color = 0xff4181EE

print(Scheme.light(color).props)
...

<details><summary>README extract</summary>

Python 3.4 added the `singledispatch` decorator to the `functools` standard library module.
Python 3.8 added the `singledispatchmethod` decorator to the `functools` standard library module,
however it does not allow sub-classes to modify the dispatch table independantly of the base class.

This library adds this functionality. ...
